巧新(1563 TT) Trading Buy

2026、 2027 年營運動能向上，投資建議 Trading Buy。  
投資建議 Trading Buy ：巧新(1563 TT) 努力進行轉型，半導體
新事業於 2H26 逐漸取得進展，且再生鋁出貨占比也明顯拉升，
帶動毛利率明顯改善。2026 年獲利表現回升且 2027 年獲利能
力續揚。惟考量近期汽車零組件族群評價不易拉升，投資建議
Trading Buy，目標價 76 元 (以 2027 年 EPS 及 PE 15 倍評價)。  
3Q26 本業獲利小幅增長，因業外收入挹注，整體獲利顯著季增；
預估 4Q26 獲利相對下降：3Q26 營收 20.16 億元，QoQ+1.83%、
YoY+18.48%。受惠於再生鋁採用比重提升且匯率相對有利，毛
利率由 2Q26 的 24.32%提升至 25.90%。但因運輸成本上漲，營
業費用同步增長，營業利益 QoQ+6.72%、 YoY+211.71%。因業
外認列大筆匯兌利益，稅率表現穩定。稅後純益 2.47 億元，
QoQ+247.56%、 YoY+478.06%， EPS 1.46 元。4Q26 方面，預估
營收 19.21 億元，QoQ-4.70%、 YoY+16.97%。獲利能力方面，
受惠於再生鋁採用率高，預估毛利率提升至 27.54%。因運費相
對下降，預估營業利益 QoQ+15.87%、 YoY+342.49%。因 3Q26
已認列大筆匯兌收益，若以保守角度看待 4Q26 業外貢獻，預估
稅後純益 1.67 億元，QoQ-32.69%、YoY 轉虧為盈，EPS 0.98 元。 
預估 2026 年獲利表現回升，2027 年獲利能力續揚：2026 年方
面，輪圈出貨小幅提升，而半導體相關零組件自 2H26 開始貢獻
初步營收。預估 2026 年營收 77.89 億元，YoY+11.63%。隨著再
生鋁採用率提升且營收規模增長，毛利率有望逐季提升，預估
2026 年毛利率提升至 24.97%，營業利益 YoY+43.13%。若業外
較無匯兌損失影響，預估稅後純益 6.16 億元，YoY+93.56%，
EPS 3.64 元。2027 年方面，根據車廠預示量，鋁圈出貨量將由
2026 年的 60 萬顆提升至 70 萬顆，有助於帶動車用產品營收增
長。而半導體營收占比有機會提升至 5~10%，將提供額外營收
動能。因再生鋁採用比重提升，且半導體產品毛利率相對較高，
2027 年毛利率及獲利能力有望優於 2026 年。預估 2027 年營收
92.77 億元，YoY+19.10%，稅後純益 8.56 億元，YoY+38.99%，
EPS 5.06 元。

  巧新(1563 TT) ESG：  
Environmental 環境保護：(1)鋁下腳料 100%回收，製成再生鋁棒，持續循環
再利用。(2)導入各式節能設備具體節能，已降低溫室氣體排放量。(3)屏東
廠自建太陽能光電。Social 社會責任：(1)獲得 ASI(鋁業管理倡議)績效標準
認證。(2)定期進行人權盡職調查與評估，流程和政策涵蓋重要的人權問題，
保障員工和活動參與者的安全。(3)響應地方政府公益活動。Governance 公
司治理：(1)風險管理依公司程序辦理，內控嚴謹，並設有專職稽核部門定期
查核。(2)將誠信經營納入新人教育訓練課程與人力資源政策中，設立明確
有效之獎懲及申訴機制。(3)每年定期發布 ESG 報告書。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Document 020: 2026 has exposed new risks for the European gas market (2026-11-09)

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026EDITORIAL
2026 HAS EXPOSED NEW RISKS FOR THE EUROPEAN GAS MARKET
1 C. Grekou, V. Mignon, L. Ragot, 2026, ‘From Russian gas to the global LNG market: The European Union’s new vulnerability’, CEPII, Policy Brief.
2 ACER, 2026, ‘Analysis of the European LNG market development. Monitoring report’.
3 These contracts allow the buyer to choose, or change, the delivery point for the shipment. Recent US contracts (Free On Board and flexible destination) generally

feature these characteristics.
4 IEA, 2026, ‘Global Gas Security Review & Gas Market Report Q4-2026’.
Europe’s vulnerability to the gas market has changed in recent years. Whilst its reliance on imports has remained stable,
its gas supply has been more vulnerable to global market conditions since 2022, due to a shift in the composition of its
imports. Furthermore, in 2026, the combination of high geopolitical risk in one of the main production regions and the

pressure of climate change on European consumers has created an unprecedented situation, leading to price pressures
and significant uncertainty regarding the short-term outlook. Even if they ease, tensions in the European gas market could
persist beyond the winter, largely due to the need to refill inventories.
Whilst Europe is not heavily reliant on gas imports from the Middle
East, it has had to contend with a fresh surge in prices since the out -

break of war in Iran last February, a rise that has, in part, been passed
on to electricity prices this summer. Furthermore, new sources of
vulnerability, particularly those linked to climate change, are at play.
These call for caution regarding the short-term outlook for the Euro -
pean gas market.
EUROPE’S GROWING EXPOSURE TO MARKET VOLATILITY
Unlike the 2022 energy crisis, which hit Europe directly, the war in
Iran is indirectly affecting the European gas market. 80% of gas exports

from the Middle East are destined for Asia. However, the growing inter-
connection between the two main gas-importing regions — Asia and
Europe — has resulted in the crisis spreading to Europe. Since 2022,
the transmission of shocks to liquefied natural gas (LNG) benchmark
prices on the European and Asian markets (TTF and JKM) has increased
significantly, in both directions1.
Despite a fall of around 20% in European gas consumption since 2022,
the replacement of around 60% of Russian pipeline imports with pur -

chases of liquefied natural gas (see Chart 1) has kept Europe’s vulne -
rability at a high level. Given developments in the LNG market —
growing interconnection between European and Asian markets and
the importance of spot contracts (around one-third of European supply
contracts2) — Europe finds itself, through no fault of its own, increa -
singly exposed to gas price volatility.
Furthermore, the increasing autonomy and flexibility of the global
LNG market may exacerbate this volatility. The higher proportion of

LNG sales contracts with flexible destinations3 (rising from 29% to 51%
of the total between 2016 and 2025), linked to the massive influx of
US LNG onto the market, together with the reduction in the share of
contracts indexed to oil prices (from 70% to 55% over the same period),
have made price formation on the LNG market more autonomous, the-
reby increasing the opportunities for arbitrage and the flexibility of this
market.
MAJOR DISRUPTIONS IN THE LNG MARKET HAVE CONSTRAINED THE REFIL-

LING OF EUROPEAN INVENTORIES
The 85% reduction in LNG exports from Qatar and the UAE in Q2 and Q3
2026 was offset by two-thirds through, on the one hand, an increase
in exports from other producing countries (primarily the United States)
and, on the other hand, a fall in demand in Asia and Europe (which
is forecast to reach, respectively, 1.5% and 3% year-on-year in 2026,
according to IEA forecasts4). Owing to the damage caused by the war to
infrastructure (17% of Qatar’s production capacity is out of service) and

logistical constraints specific to the LNG market (unavailability of LNG
carriers), the LNG market has benefited only marginally from the re -
sumption of flows through the Strait of Hormuz since July. Against this
backdrop of tensions in the gas market, high prices in Europe (up by
around 174% since the outbreak of the conflict) have significantly
slowed the refilling of European stocks. They stood at 75% of full capa-
city at the end of October, whilst the official target is 80% (see Chart 2).

OVER THE WINTER, DIVERGENT FACTORS COULD STABILISE PRICES AT A
HIGH LEVEL
Gas stock levels that are below the target set by the European Commis-
sion do not increase the risk of a shortage on the European continent.
Nevertheless, Europe’s vulnerability towards the end of winter, in the
event of a cold snap, would force it to buy on the market at high prices.

for a changing
world
Furthermore, from a technical perspective, exposure to the conse -
quences of a cold snap at the end of winter is all the more pronounced
as the rate at which gas can be withdrawn from storage (the ease with
which a country can access its supplies) decreases (due to lower stock
levels in storage facilities).
Apart from stock levels at the start of winter, three factors will deter -

mine the situation at the end of the season, a key period of vulnera -
bility:
1/ the resumption of exports from the Gulf, a region that is essential for
restoring market balance in the short term;
2/ weather conditions during the winter;
3/ renewable energy production.
According to the IEA, in the worst-case scenario (with exports from the
Middle East only resuming from the end of the first quarter of 2027, a
cold winter and low renewable energy production), stocks would stand

at 7% of total capacity by the end of winter, compared with 33% in
the best-case scenario. Such levels remain significantly below the ave-
rage for the last five years at this time of year: 80% in the worst-case
scenario and 18% in the best-case scenario. This would underpin res -
tocking demand over the next two quarters, and therefore keep prices
at a fairly high level. For the time being, a significant recovery in LNG
exports from the Gulf remains highly uncertain, even though indirect

talks between the warring parties have resumed. On a more positive
note, the current development of a very powerful El Niño event should,
in principle, be associated with relatively mild temperatures during the
winter in western Europe. According to the futures markets, European
gas prices are not expected to return to their pre-crisis levels until
2028 (see Chart 3).
ARE WE HEADING FOR A SUMMER PEAK IN GAS DEMAND?
In addition to these factors, which are likely to prolong market tensions

during the first half of 2027, the summer period could also be cha -
racterised by increased demand for gas. During the summer of 2026,
5 In early October 2026, the water levels in French reservoirs reached a ten-year low, standing at 23% below seasonal averages.
6 RTE data.
7 M. Mueller, P. Devaux, October 2026, ‘What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity
continues to turn the screw?’, EcoInsight.

8 Oxford Institute for Energy Studies, October 2026, Quarterly Gas Market Review.
weather conditions hampered the generation of certain forms of re -
newable energy (heatwaves and droughts restricting electricity gene -
ration from hydroelectric5 and wind power sources). They also disrup-
ted nuclear power generation (in France, nuclear electricity generation
fell by around 10% year-on-year between August and October6). Along-
side these constraints on carbon-free electricity generation, heatwaves

are driving up demand for electricity, particularly for air conditioning.
Against this backdrop, as a source of flexibility within the electricity
mix, gas remains a key determinant of electricity prices in Europe 7.
In Q3 2026, whilst the decline in European gas consumption mainly
affected industry, the residential sector and the commercial sector 8,
consumption by gas-fired power stations was 9% higher than in 2025.
STRENGTHENING EUROPEAN PROGRAMMES

In this context, and despite the progress made in decarbonising the
European electricity mix, high gas prices have led to a rise in whole -
sale electricity prices in most European countries. Between July and
September 2026, these prices were on average 54% higher than in the
same period a year earlier.
Against this backdrop of widespread rises in energy prices, European go-
vernments have primarily introduced measures to support households’
purchasing power when buying fuel. Measures relating to gas supply

remain fairly limited. The European Commission has extended the tar-
get for refilling European stocks to 80% (instead of 90%) for 2026. It has
also urged Member States to extend the stock-refill season in order
to smooth out purchase prices. Furthermore, the measures taken in
2022 under the RePower EU programme have been maintained: redu -
cing dependence on Russian gas with the aim of a complete phase-out
(pipeline and LNG) by the end of 2027; and pooling gas purchases at

European level. The ‘decarbonisation and electrification’ component of
this programme was strengthened in April 2026 by the Accelerate EU
programme, notably through measures to accelerate the electrification
of demand and the development of electricity grid infrastructure at
European level.

At national level, it is worth highlighting the price cap mechanism for
gas purchased by power stations, which the Italian government is set
to introduce. In 2025, gas set the price of electricity in Italy in 73% of
cases (compared with an average of 40% across Europe). This price cap
mechanism, similar to that introduced in Spain in 2022, is, in principle,

only sustainable in the short term. Indeed, its cost is likely to be signi-
ficant given the Italian electricity mix’s high dependence on gas.
*****
Increased market dependence and the consequences of rising climate
risk for European electricity generation are altering the vulnerabilities
associated with the continent’s gas market. The sources of risk are
increasing in number and becoming harder to manage. This calls for
the continuation and strengthening of decarbonisation efforts in the

European policies launched in 2022.
Pascal Devaux
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

2026 年 11 月 17 日

調査レポート

2026 年 7～9 月期の GDP（1 次速報）結果

～個人消費が力強さを欠き、4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長～

調査部 主任研究員 藤田隼平

2026 年 11 月 16 日に内閣府が公表した 7～9 月期の実質 GDP（1 次速報値）は、前期比－0.0%（年率換算   
－0.1%）と減少した。設備投資が前期比＋1.6%と高めの伸びとなった一方、個人消費が同－0.3%と 2 四半期連
続で減少したことなどが下押し要因となり、小幅ながら 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。なお、輸入が鉱物
性燃料を中心に持ち直したことで外需寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスとなったが、公的在庫の寄与
度が＋0.5%ポイントとなり、おおむね相殺された。 
過去 3 四半期にわたって潜在成長率を上回る高めの伸びが続いていたことや、設備投資が堅調に増加したこ
とを踏まえると、今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の動きが途切れたとは判断できないが、景気が力強さを欠
いていることも事実だろう。特に GDP の過半を占める個人消費に弱さがみられたことは懸念材料である。今後は、
良好な雇用・所得環境に支えられて個人消費は持ち直しへ向かうと期待されるが、消費者物価の伸び拡大や住
宅ローン金利の上昇による家計負担の増加を通じて、消費の減少が続くリスクには警戒が必要である。 
なお、物価上昇分を含んだ名目 GDP は、前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.6%）と、10 四半期連続のプラス成長
となった。物価上昇圧力は依然として強く、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進む中で、GDP デフレーターは前年比  
＋2.4%（季節調整済前期比では＋0.4%）と、高い伸びが続いた。

（注）内需寄与度、民間在庫、外需寄与度は実質 GDP に対する寄与度（%ポイント）

 1 次速報値を踏まえた、2026 年 7～9 月期における需要項目別の動向（実質ベース）は以下の通り。

① 実質個人消費（前期比－0.3%） 
 家計を取り巻く所得環境は改善した（実質雇用者報酬は前期比＋0.3%）が、前期に自動車や
エアコンが一時的な要因によって増加した反動で耐久財が前期比－3.5%と減少した上、夏場
の地震や台風、長雨などの自然災害・天候不順によって消費の機会に恵まれなかったこと、食
料品の値上げや住宅ローン金利の上昇など今後の生活不安につながる話題も多く節約志向が
強まったことから、半耐久財や非耐久財も減少した。ただし、サービスは 9 月のシルバーウイー
クの日並びが良かったこともあり、小幅に増加した。 
② 実質住宅投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 住宅投資に先行する住宅着工戸数が 2 四半期ぶりに減少する中、建築コストの増加も逆風とな
り、住宅投資は 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
③ 実質設備投資（前期比＋1.6%） 
 企業の省力化や生産性向上に向けた投資意欲は強く、特に 7～9 月期はサーバーや半導体製
造装置といった AI 関連投資も好調だったことで、設備投資は 3 四半期ぶりに増加した。 
④ 実質民間在庫（前期比寄与度＋0.0%ポイント） 
 前期に大きめのプラス寄与となったこともあり、民間在庫の寄与は横ばいとなった。 
 なお、公的在庫は国家備蓄原油の減少幅が縮小したことから、前期比寄与度＋0.5%ポイントと
なった。 
⑤ 実質政府最終消費（前期比－0.0%） 
 前期に高めの伸びとなったこともあり、横ばいだった。 
⑥ 実質公共投資（前期比－1.1%） 
 名目では増加したものの、工事コストの増加を受けたデフレーターの上昇に追いついておらず、
実質では 2 四半期連続で減少した。 
⑦ 外需（前期比寄与度－0.6%ポイント、実質輸出：前期比－0.2%、実質輸入：同＋2.5%） 
 輸出は前期比－0.2%と減少した。財は堅調な米国向けや中東向けの持ち直しを受けて増加し
た一方、サービスは前期に研究開発サービスが一時的な要因により上振れた反動で減少した。 
 輸入は前期比＋2.5%と増加した。特に財が鉱物性燃料を中心に持ち直した。ただし、サービス
については減少した。 
 結果、輸出から輸入を差し引いた外需の寄与度は－0.6%ポイントと大きめのマイナスになった。

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

研 究 報 告                
穩懋(3105.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
2027 光通訊跟衛星本業雙引擎 認列聯茂收益業外動能強勁 
 投資評價與建議 
公司過往Forward PER落在20~30倍，考量光通訊與衛星業務出貨放量，毛利
率迎來結構性跳升，有利估值重估，維持「增加持股」評等。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收符合預期，毛利率因產品組合優化上升至37.4%：3Q26營收58.6億
元，季增11%，營收符合預期主要係因傳統消費性電子旺季，加上航太衛星與
光電產品動能強勁，毛利率37.4%，季增9.2個百分點，主要係因產品組合優化，
航太衛星與光電產品營收占比過半，加上產能利用率上升至75%，業外收入
6.98億元，主因聯茂貢獻業外EPS約1.61元，稅後EPS 4.55元。 
4Q26營運淡季不淡，2027成⻑動能聚焦Optical跟Infrastructure：(1)4Q26
面臨手機傳統淡季與年底例行性歲修，但受惠於PD產能持續開出與其他產品
線營收持平至微幅成⻑，預估4Q26營收將季增1~3%，毛利率37~39%。(2)光
通訊PD於3Q26逐步放量，4Q26將持續成⻑，預估2027年大幅放量，CW Laser
正進行可靠度測試，預估2027年小量貢獻，顯著營收爆發期落在2028年，EML
處於產品開發與測試階段，2028年才有機會產生營收貢獻。(3)航太衛星帶動
Infrastructure成為3Q26季增幅度最高的產品線，衛星佔Infrastructure營收
比重已升至40%，超越傳統基地台。(4)預估聯茂2027年EPS 72.22元，穩懋採
權益法認列持股23%之投資收益約61.7億元，貢獻EPS約14.56元。 
 投資風險 
光通PD跟雷射放量不如預期、衛星發射遞延。

| 營運概況 
3Q26 產品組合優化帶動毛利率與 EPS 創高：3Q26 營收 58.6 億元，季增 11%，營收符合預期主要係因傳統消費
性電子旺季，加上航太衛星與光電產品動能強勁，毛利率 37.4%，季增 9.2 個百分點，優於財測，主要係因產品
組合優化，航太衛星與光電產品營收占比過半，加上產能利用率上升至 75%，營業利益率 24.2%，業外收入 6.98
億元，因持有聯茂 23%股權並採權益法認列投資收益，聯茂貢獻業外 EPS 約 1.61 元，稅後 EPS 4.55 元。 
4Q26 營運淡季不淡，2027 成⻑動能聚焦 Optical 跟 Infrastructure：(1)儘管 4Q26 面臨手機傳統淡季與年底例
行性歲修，但受惠於 PD 產能持續開出與其他產品線營收持平至微幅成⻑，預估 4Q26 營收將季增 1~3%，毛利
率 37~39%，主要受惠產品組合優化跟產能利用率維持 70%以上。(2)中階手機受臨中國 PA 供應鏈本土化及記憶
體缺貨的逆風，但穩懋聚焦中高階手機市場，動能維持穩健，4Q26 Cellular 營收僅呈現小幅下滑，WiFi 產品在
Router 需求回流下，3Q26 雙位數成⻑，4Q26 呈現持平至微幅成⻑。(3)光通訊 PD 於 3Q26 逐步放量，4Q26
將持續成⻑，預估 2027 年產能完全開出並大幅放量，CW Laser 正進行可靠度測試，預估 2027 年小量貢獻，顯
著營收爆發期落在 2028，EML 處於產品開發與測試階段，2028 年才有機會產生營收貢獻。(4)航太衛星帶動
Infrastructure 成為 3Q26 季增幅度最高的產品線，衛星佔 Infrastructure 營收比重已升至 40%，超越傳統基地
台，目前 GaN 月產能是 300 片左右，公司積極擴充 GaN 產能，預計 1Q27 導入 GaN 6 吋製程生產。(5)聯茂
2027 年爆發性成⻑，預估聯茂 2027 年 EPS 72.22 元，穩懋採權益法認列持股 23%之投資收益約 61.7 億元，貢
獻EPS約14.56元。

| 公 司 簡 介 
穩懋(3105)為全球最大的化合物半導體晶圓代工廠，核心業務聚焦於微波通訊與光電元件的砷化鎵(GaAs)及氮化
鎵(GaN)晶圓代工服務，公司總部及研發中心設於台灣桃園龜山(華亞科技園區)，主要六吋晶圓生產基地位於桃
園華亞廠及龜山廠，總產能約 4.3 萬片，3Q26 產能利用率為 75%。3Q26 年營收比重為 Cellular(手機
PA)25~30%、Infrastructure(通訊基礎建設)35~40%、Wi-Fi 15~20%、Optical 10~15%、其他 3%。主要客
戶包含 Broadcom、Lumentum、Murata 等國際大廠，銷售地區為台灣 8%、亞洲 70%、美洲 15%、歐洲
7%。 
穩懋的微波通訊產品處理高頻訊號的放大與切換，主要元件為異質接面雙載子電晶體(HBT)與假晶高電子遷移率
電晶體(pHEMT) ，用於智慧型手機功率放大器(PA)、Wi-Fi 路由器及低軌衛星設備的射頻(RF)訊號，提供低雜

1 
3597 映興(AVIN)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：洪瑞陽 (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8% ，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

映興在 AI 資料中心的供應模式以電源系統相關線束及連接元件為

主，並非直接供應 CSP，因此營運成長主要隨下游客戶資料中心
電源系統出貨放大。本研究預期 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，
2027 年除具全年化效果外，出貨仍持續 Ramp，因此預估新專案
相關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2025 年的約四成提高至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年約 65%，成為未
來兩年主要營收增量來源。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5% ；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack 、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

新增營收有助於產能利用率及固定成本攤提改善，本研究預估四
季毛利率分別為 23.55%／ 23.43%／ 23.35%／ 23.25%，全年約
23.39%。

營業費用方面，本研究依 3Q26 實際費用及 4Q26 預估費用水準進
行推估，考量營運規模擴大，預估 1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 營
業費用分別為 5,200／5,400／5,600／5,820 萬元，全年約 2.20 億
元，YoY 約+20.8%；惟營收增幅高於費用，預估全年費用率由 2026
年的 15.5%降至 12.4%，帶動營業利益約 1.95 元、營業利益率
11.0%。

業外收入預估約 500 萬元，有效稅率維持 35%，預估 2027 年稅後
淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.33 元，YoY 約+104%。季度 EPS 預估分
別為 0.74／0.80／0.87／0.93 元，營業利益率隨營收放量由 1Q27
的 10.7%逐步提高至 4Q27 的 11.3%，顯示本輪獲利成長仍主要由
營業槓桿帶動。 
三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

### Page 4

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

4 
[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 29 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 
본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 자료를 
기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 09 월 30 일)

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(YoY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(YoY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(YoY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(YoY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(YoY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(YoY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

Document 255: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports.1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.
Exhibit 1: FDI Inflows to the U.S.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

from the upcoming electoral cycle

Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026,2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Document 306: Claude证明黎曼假设“大反转”：论证无效，只是空转？

Source PDF: all report pdf/Claude证明黎曼假设“大反转”：论证无效，只是空转？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

11 ⽉ 20 ⽇， Anthropic 数学家 Alpöge 宣称与 Claude 证明黎曼假设，公开 1700 万⾏ Lean 代
码。但数学家 Nielsen 同⽇发⽂指出， Alpöge“ 发布的内容不包含黎曼假设的证明。所提证
明⽆效 ” 。她认为，这个证明 “ 形式通过、实质为空 ” 。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 20 ⽇， Anthropic 数学家 Levent Alpöge 在个⼈⽹站发布说明，宣布⾃⼰与 Claude 共同找到
了黎曼假设的证明。
他没有交出⼈类可读的论⽂，⽽是直接放出⼀个 Lean 4 代码库： 175,187 个⽂件、约 1700 万⾏
代码。黎曼假设 1859 年由 Bernhard Riemann 提出，是克雷数学研究所悬赏 100 万美元的千禧年
问题之⼀，⾄今已悬置约 167 年。
次⽇，Lean Focused Research Organization 与 Mathlib Initiative 联合表⽰： “ 我们有充分信
⼼认为， Alpöge 关于黎曼假设的证明是正确的。 ”据《卫报》报道，两家机构是在审阅其 Lean 代

码后作出这⼀表态的。 MIT 数学家 Andrew Sutherland 也独⽴编译了该结果，他说： “ 这确实是⼀
个历史性的时刻，世界已经改变。 ”
然⽽就在同⼀天，另⼀份⽂稿出现了。
数学家 J. L. Nielsen （署名单位为堪萨斯州 Center for Topological Physics ）于 11 ⽉ 20 ⽇发布论
⽂《 Alpöge 所提黎曼假设证明的⽆效性》。她的判断⾮常直接： “ 发布内容不包含黎曼假设的证
明。所提证明⽆效。 ”
反转：⼏何条件是 “ 永远为真 ”
Nielsen 检查的是那份 175,187 个⽂件的 Lean 归档，逐条追踪顶层定理的证明项依赖。她认为
Lean 算对了，但算的东西是空的。
Lean 是 “ 证明助⼿ ” ，它不判断⼀个数学想法⾼不⾼级，只检查⼀件事：从你写下的定义出发，能
不能⼀步步推到结论。
Nielsen 翻查源码后发现，证明路径中需要⼀类 “ ⼏何对象 ” 作为⻅证（ witness ，即⽤来⽀撑某
个论断的具体例⼦）。但代码⾥这个条件是这样写的：

anyGeometry : GeoP := fun _ _ _ => True
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

翻译成⼈话就是：不管输⼊什么，⼀律返回 “ 成⽴ ” 。
第⼀处是⼏何条件。代码中有⼀个名为anyGeometry的谓词，定义为fun _ _ _ => True
—— ⽆论输⼊什么，它都返回 “ 真 ” 。按论⽂的说法，这个定义在 24 个⽂件中被使⽤了 118 次，其
中包括最终证明链上的关键 “ ⻅证 ” （ witness ，即论证中需要举出的具体对象）。
第⼆处是⼏何对象本⾝。代码⽤⼀个名为geoDegenerate的 “ 退化模型 ” 充当⼏何⻅证：体积为
0 、范数为 0 、曲线退化成⼀个点、曲⾯类型为空集。所有正性条件（如 nef 、 big ）都被定义为
True。论⽂写道，这个模型 “ 只携带⼀个旗流形的度数数字，背后没有任何簇 ” 。
第三处是⾼度函数。证明需要某个⼏何对象具有⼀个 “ ⾼度 ” （ height ，可粗略理解为衡量对象复杂
程度的数值），并被夹在上下界之间。但代码⾥，这个⾼度先被设为 0 ，随后被直接改写成⼀个
显式公式hExpl6，于是 “ ⾼度 = 公式 ” 这⼀步由rfl（⾃反性，即 “ 因为定义如此 ” ）⾃动成⽴。论

⽂的原话是：⾼度 “ 不是从任何⼏何对象推导出来的，⽽是⼀个公式，被选中以让论证闭合 ” 。
Nielsen 认为，⼏何侧与解析侧被这三步强制成了同⼀个对象，本该 “ 夹住 ” 独⽴⼏何⾼度的不等
式，实际是 “ ⼀个量和它⾃⼰⽐较 ” 。
Nielsen 的结论是：这些条件属于 “ 空真（ vacuously true ） ”—— 她写道： “ 形式化所证明的只是
同 义 反 复 —— 黎 曼 假 设 等 于 黎 曼 假 设 。 ” 即 形 式 上 为 真 ， 但 没 有 断 ⾔ 任 何 实 质 内 容 。 （ The
proposed proof is invalid: what has been checked is a term whose structural witnesses are
vacuously true, not the argument those witnesses stand for. ）。
所提出的证明是⽆效的：所检验的是⼀个结构性证据空洞成⽴的术语，⽽不是这些证据所代表的

论点。

### Page 3

⽀持者：内核通过就是通过
另⼀边的声⾳同样响亮。
Lean 与 Mathlib 社 区 的 验 证 针 对 的 是 另 ⼀ 组 问 题 ： 顶 层 定 理 是 否 匹 配 Mathlib 中 标 准 的
RiemannHypothesis定义、代码能否编译、多个独⽴检查器是否接受。这些检查全部通过，
且代码中没有axiom（额外公理）、没有sorry（占位符）、没有native_decide（绕过内核的
判定）。
在他们看来，这已经⾜够：⼀个类型正确的封闭项，就是该陈述的证明。
Polymarket 在 11 ⽉ 22 ⽇发帖概括了这场争议： “ 新分析声称 AI 辅助的黎曼假设证明⽆效，尽管它
已在 Lean 中成功验证。 ” 这条帖⼦获得 76.8 万次浏览，评论区迅速分裂。
⼤量反驳指向 Nielsen 本⼈。有⽤⼾翻出她的 PhilPapers 记录，称她此前对 OpenAI 的 Yang-
Mills 证明、 Navier-Stokes 证明都发过类似 “ ⽆效 ” 论⽂；也有⼈指出 “Center for Topological

Physics” 的出版物列表上只有她⼀⼈。⼀条⾼赞评论写道： “Lean 说有效，⼀位数学家说⽆效，
你赌谁？ ”

### Page 4

也有技术性反驳。有⽤⼾称，尼尔森 “ 犯了⼀个新⼿错误：以为恒真的前提可以平凡地推出黎曼
假设 ”—— ⾔下之意，恒真前提只能让那⼀步⽆内容，不能⾃动让最终结论失效。

### Page 5

争议的核⼼：定义算不算数学
这场争论真正卡住的地⽅，是形式验证的两个层次被混在了⼀起。
第⼀层：在给定定义和公理下，代码是否导出了⽬标定理？ Lean 社区的回答是肯定的。
第⼆层：这些定义是否忠实对应了研究者想表达的数学内容？ Nielsen 的回答是否定的。
⽤⼤⽩话说： Lean 能保证 “ 从你写下的规则出发，推理没有⼀步⾛错 ” ，但不能保证 “ 你写下的规
则就是你想说的那件事 ” 。如果规则⾥埋了⼀句 “ ⼏何条件永远成⽴ ” ，那么依赖它的推理再严
密，也没有真正触及⼏何。
有媒体的报道概括称：争议 “ 并⾮单纯指控 Lean 编译器出错，⽽是针对证明中部分数学对象的定
义提出异议 ” ；同时强调， Nielsen 的论⽂ “ 仍属可接受公开检验与反驳的研究主张，⽽⾮数学界
已取得共识的最终判决 ” 。
⼈类可读版本的缺席
还有⼀个细节让争论难以收场：没有论⽂。
传统数学证明，同⾏可以逐⾏阅读、质疑、重写。⽽这次，外界⾯对的是⼀座 1700 万⾏的代码
库。 Nielsen 在论⽂中说明，她读了约 70 个⽂件，多数是全⽂，聚焦顶层⽂件与第 1 ⾄ 7 节点名的

定义和引理 —— 相对于 175,187 个⽂件，这是⼀个极⼩的采样。
这意味着双⽅其实都还没有完成 “ ⼈类层⾯的消化 ” 。 MIT 数学家 Andrew Sutherland 在验证通过
当天就说： “ 要理解证明的数学细节，还有很多⼯作要做。我迫不及待想钻研进去。 ”

### Page 6

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Document 333: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

### Page 14

14
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
From Follow the Money and Greenberg Center for Geoeconomics
export. The world has never encountered such elastic
supply.
**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy
differences (TRQs vs. a flat tariff, for example) the

harder it would be to ever develop a harmonized
policy approach. But with President Trump, a
harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

Document 340: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

### Page 3

The bank for
a changing
world

Document 344: Germany’s hidden advantage in the AI race

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany’s hidden advantage in the AI race.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Berlin may be Germany’s innovation powerhouse, but across
Europe, the country is still playing catch-up. When it comes to
AI, however, it could have much more up its sleeve than its
overall innovation performance suggests

Franziska Biehl and Carsten Brzeski
Once we turn from traditional innovation to artiﬁcial intelligence, Germany
suddenly looks much more competitive
Germany’s innovation problem runs deep…
Germany’s innovation landscape remains highly fragmented. Berlin
tops our national Innovation Index for the 12th consecutive year,
followed by Hamburg and Baden-Württemberg, while Thuringia,
Mecklenburg-Western Pomerania and Saxony-Anhalt remain at the
bottom, held back by demographic pressures, skills shortages and
weak business creation.
These regional diﬀerences reﬂect the structural challenges holding
back innovation capacity across the country. Consequently,
Germany only ranks 16th out of the 21 eurozone countries in our
ING Germany Innovation Index for the eurozone, leaving one of
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
